(b) shows the relative error between the binodal densities and their asymptotic values at large Péclet, decreasing to zero when
{\rm Pe}\to\infty
.

3
Linear stability analysis for
{\rm MPS}>1

For soft-core rAPM, the hydrodynamic equation is

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{\sigma}=D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}\left[(1-\zeta\rho)\partial_{\parallel}\rho_{\sigma}+\zeta\rho_{\sigma}\partial_{\parallel}\rho\right]+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}\left[(1-\zeta\rho)\partial_{\perp}\rho_{\sigma}+\zeta\rho_{\sigma}\partial_{\perp}\rho\right]-v\partial_{\parallel}\left[(1-\zeta\rho)\rho_{\sigma}\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\left[\frac{4\beta J}{\rho}(\rho_{\sigma}+\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1-\frac{r}{\rho}-\alpha\frac{(\rho_{\sigma}-\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right](\rho_{\sigma}-\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}),

(124)

with
\alpha=8(\beta J)^{2}(1-2\beta J/3)
and
\zeta=1/{\rm MPS}
. The homogeneous solutions are given by:

I_{\rm flip}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})=\left[\frac{4\beta J}{\rho}(\rho_{\sigma}+\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1-\frac{r}{\rho}-\alpha\frac{(\rho_{\sigma}-\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right](\rho_{\sigma}-\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})=0,

(125)

and are then those of the unrestricted rAPM
1
. The disordered homogeneous solution is
\rho_{\sigma}=\rho_{0}/4
, and the ordered homogeneous solution (supposed along state
\sigma=1
) is
\rho_{1}=\rho_{0}(1+3M)/4
and
\rho_{2,3,4}=\rho_{0}(1-M)/4
with the magnetization
M
following the equation:

2\beta J(1+M)-1-\frac{r}{\rho_{0}}-\alpha M^{2}=0,

(126)

or
M=M_{0}\pm M_{1}\delta
with
M_{0}=\beta J/\alpha
,
M_{1}=\sqrt{r/\alpha\rho_{*}}
and
\delta=\sqrt{(\rho_{0}-\rho_{*})/\rho_{0}}
, where
\rho_{*}
defined by

\rho_{*}=\frac{8(1-2\beta J/3)r}{1+8(2\beta J-1)(1-2\beta J/3)},

(127)

is the critical density below which the ordered homogeneous solution does not exist, for a temperature below
T_{c}=(1-\sqrt{22}/8)^{-1}\simeq 2.417
.

Linear stability analysis for the disordered homogeneous solution.
We take
\rho_{\sigma}=\rho_{0}/4+\delta\rho_{\sigma}
and
\rho=\rho_{0}+\delta\rho
, with
\delta\rho=\sum_{\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma}
. The hopping term writes

\displaystyle I_{\rm hop}\simeq\left[\left(1-\frac{3\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}\right)(D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2})-v\left(1-\frac{5\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}\right)\partial_{\parallel}\right]\delta\rho_{\sigma}

\displaystyle+\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}\left[D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2}+v\partial_{\parallel}\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},

(128)

and the flipping term writes
I_{\rm flip}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\simeq\mu_{0}(\delta\rho_{\sigma}-\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})
, with
\mu_{0}=2\beta J-1-r/\rho_{0}
. Then, in the Fourier space, the hydrodynamic equation becomes

\partial_{t}\delta\rho_{\sigma}=\left[A(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})+3\mu_{0}\right]\delta\rho_{\sigma}+\left[B(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})-\mu_{0}\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},

(129)

with

\displaystyle A(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\left(1-\frac{3\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}\right)(-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2})+\imath k_{\parallel}v\left(1-\frac{5\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}\right)

(130)

\displaystyle B(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}\left[-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}-\imath k_{\parallel}v\right]

(131)

The stability of the homogeneous disordered solution is then given by the eigenvalues of the matrix

M_{\rm gas}=\begin{pmatrix}A(k_{x},k_{y})+3\mu_{0}&B(k_{x},k_{y})-\mu_{0}&B(k_{x},k_{y})-\mu_{0}&B(k_{x},k_{y})-\mu_{0}\\
B(k_{y},-k_{x})-\mu_{0}&A(k_{y},-k_{x})+3\mu_{0}&B(k_{y},-k_{x})-\mu_{0}&B(k_{y},-k_{x})-\mu_{0}\\
B(-k_{x},-k_{y})-\mu_{0}&B(-k_{x},-k_{y})-\mu_{0}&A(-k_{x},-k_{y})+3\mu_{0}&B(-k_{x},-k_{y})-\mu_{0}\\
B(-k_{y},k_{x})-\mu_{0}&B(-k_{y},k_{x})-\mu_{0}&B(-k_{y},k_{x})-\mu_{0}&A(-k_{y},k_{x})+3\mu_{0}\end{pmatrix}.

(132)

Supposing
k_{x}=k
and
k_{y}=0
(since no preferred direction), at leading order in
k\ll 1
, the eigenvalues, calculated with Mathematica
2
, are:
\lambda_{\rm gas}^{1,2,3}\simeq 4\mu_{0}
and

\lambda_{\rm gas}^{4}\simeq\left[-\frac{D_{\parallel}+D_{\perp}}{2}+(1-\zeta\rho_{0})(1-2\zeta\rho_{0})\frac{v^{2}}{8\mu_{0}}\right]k^{2}.

(133)

The disordered homogeneous solution is then stable if
\mu_{0}<0
and

\lambda_{\rm gas}=-D+(1-\zeta\rho_{0})(1-2\zeta\rho_{0})\frac{v^{2}}{8\mu_{0}}<0.

(134)

Figure 76:
(Color online)
(a)
and
(b)
Velocity-density stability diagram of the rAPM with hard-core repulsion for the disordered and ordered homogeneous solution for
\beta=0.75
for
{\rm MPS}=2
.
(a)
The stability region of the disordered solution is plotted in blue, according to the eigenvalue
\lambda_{\rm gas}
given by Eq. (
134
).
(b)
The stability region of the ordered solution is plotted in blue, according to the eigenvalues
\lambda_{\parallel}
and
\lambda_{\perp}
given by Eqs. (
149
) and (
150
), respectively. The ordered solution is only unstable under longitudinal perturbations (
\lambda_{\parallel}>0
) in the light blue region, and under transverse perturbations (
\lambda_{\perp}>0
) in the light red region.
(c)

\epsilon_{*}
value for which the reorientation transition occurs in the rAPM with MPS
>
, as a function of MPS for several temperatures
T=\beta^{-1}
.

In Fig.
76
(a), we have represented the velocity-density stability diagram for
\beta=0.75
and
{\rm MPS}=2
according to the sign of this eigenvalue. Note that the last inequality is always fulfilled when
\zeta=0
, meaning that
\mu_{0}<0
only impacts the spinodals:
\varphi_{\rm gas}(\zeta=0)=r/(2\beta J-1)
, independent of
\epsilon
. However, we cannot extract an analytical expression for the spinodal
\varphi_{\rm gas}
for all
{\rm MPS}
values.

Linear stability analysis for the ordered homogeneous solution.
We consider the ordered solution along the right state, and we take
\rho_{1}=\rho_{0}(1+3M)/4+\delta\rho_{1}
,
\rho_{2,3,4}=\rho_{0}(1+3M)/4+\delta\rho_{2,3,4}
and
\rho=\rho_{0}+\delta\rho
, with
\delta\rho=\sum_{\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma}
. The hopping term of the right state
\sigma=1
writes

\displaystyle I_{\rm hop}^{(1)}\simeq\left\{\left[1-\frac{3\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(1-M)\right](D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2})-v\left[1-\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(5+3M)\right]\partial_{\parallel}\right\}\delta\rho_{\sigma}

\displaystyle+\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(1+3M)\left[D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2}+v\partial_{\parallel}\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},

(135)

and the hopping term of the other states
\sigma\neq 1
writes

\displaystyle I_{\rm hop}^{(2)}\simeq\left\{\left[1-\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(3+M)\right](D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2})-v\left[1-\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(5-M)\right]\partial_{\parallel}\right\}\delta\rho_{\sigma}

\displaystyle+\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(1-M)\left[D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2}+v\partial_{\parallel}\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}.

(136)

We may note for
M=0
, we recover the expression of
I_{\rm hop}
, given by Eq. (
128
), calculated for the disordered solution.

The flipping terms implying the right state write

I_{\rm flip}(1,\sigma^{\prime})\simeq M\left\{(4\beta J-2\alpha M)\delta\rho_{1}+(4\beta J+2\alpha M)\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}-\left[2\beta J(1+M)-\frac{r}{\rho_{0}}-2\alpha M^{2}\right]\delta\rho\right\}.

(137)

Using Eq. (
126
), we get

\displaystyle I_{\rm flip}(1,\sigma^{\prime})\simeq M\left[(4\beta J-2\alpha M)\delta\rho_{1}+(4\beta J+2\alpha M)\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}+(\alpha M^{2}-1)\delta\rho\right]

\displaystyle\equiv\gamma_{1}\delta\rho_{1}+\gamma_{2}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}+\gamma_{3}\delta\rho.

(138)

The flipping terms which does not imply the right state write

I_{\rm flip}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\simeq M(\alpha M-4\beta J)(\delta\rho_{\sigma}-\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})\equiv\gamma_{4}(\delta\rho_{\sigma}-\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}).

(139)

Then we have the terms:

\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}I_{\rm flip}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\simeq\begin{cases}3(\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{3})\delta\rho_{\sigma}+(\gamma_{2}+3\gamma_{3})\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},\qquad&{\rm if}\penalty\ \sigma=1,\\
-(\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{3})\delta\rho_{1}+(-\gamma_{2}-\gamma_{3}+2\gamma_{4})\delta\rho_{\sigma}-(\gamma_{3}+\gamma_{4})\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\{1,\sigma\}}\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},\qquad&{\rm if}\penalty\ \sigma\neq 1.\end{cases}

(140)

Then, in the Fourier space, the hydrodynamic equation becomes

\partial_{t}\delta\rho_{\sigma}=\begin{cases}\left[A_{1}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})+3\mu\right]\delta\rho_{\sigma}+\left[B_{1}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})+\nu\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},\hskip 18.49988pt&{\rm if}\penalty\ \sigma=1,\\
\left[A_{2}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})+\kappa\right]\delta\rho_{\sigma}+\left[B_{2}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})-\mu\right]\delta\rho_{1}+\left[B_{2}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})-\frac{\kappa+\nu}{2}\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\{1,\sigma\}}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},\hskip 18.49988pt&{\rm if}\penalty\ \sigma\neq 1,\end{cases}

(141)

with
\mu=\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{3}=M(4\beta J-2\alpha M+\alpha M^{2}-1)
,
\nu=\gamma_{2}+3\gamma_{3}=M(4\beta J+2\alpha M+3\alpha M^{2}-3)
,
\kappa=-\gamma_{2}-\gamma_{3}+2\gamma_{4}=M(-12\beta J-\alpha M^{2}+1)
, and

\displaystyle A_{1}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\left[1-\frac{3\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(1-M)\right](-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2})+\imath k_{\parallel}v\left[1-\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(5+3M)\right],

(142)

\displaystyle B_{1}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(1+3M)\left[-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}-\imath k_{\parallel}v\right],

(143)

\displaystyle A_{2}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\left[1-\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(3+M)\right](-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2})+\imath k_{\parallel}v\left[1-\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(5-M)\right],

(144)

\displaystyle B_{2}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\frac{\zeta\rho_{0}}{4}(1-M)\left[-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}-\imath k_{\parallel}v\right].

(145)

The stability of the homogeneous ordered solution is then given by the eigenvalues of the matrix

M_{\rm liq}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{1}(k_{x},k_{y})+3\mu&B_{1}(k_{x},k_{y})+\nu&B_{1}(k_{x},k_{y})+\nu&B_{1}(k_{x},k_{y})+\nu\\
B_{2}(k_{y},-k_{x})-\mu&A_{2}(k_{y},-k_{x})+\kappa&B_{2}(k_{y},-k_{x})-(\kappa+\nu)/2&B_{2}(k_{y},-k_{x})-(\kappa+\nu)/2\\
B_{2}(-k_{x},-k_{y})-\mu&B_{2}(-k_{x},-k_{y})-(\kappa+\nu)/2&A_{2}(-k_{x},-k_{y})+\kappa&B_{2}(-k_{x},-k_{y})-(\kappa+\nu)/2\\
B_{2}(-k_{y},k_{x})-\mu&B_{2}(-k_{y},k_{x})-(\kappa+\nu)/2&B_{2}(-k_{y},k_{x})-(\kappa+\nu)/2&A_{2}(-k_{y},k_{x})+\kappa\end{pmatrix}.

(146)

First, we consider a perturbation in the
x
direction (
k_{y}=0
). At leading order in
k_{x}\ll 1
, the real part of the eigenvalues, calculated with Mathematica
2
, are:
\lambda_{\rm liq,x}^{1,2}=(3\kappa+\nu)/2
,
\lambda_{\rm liq,x}^{3}\simeq(3\mu+\nu)
, and

\displaystyle\lambda_{{\rm liq},x}^{4}\simeq-\left[\frac{(D_{\parallel}+2D_{\perp})\mu-D_{\parallel}\nu}{3\mu-\nu}+\frac{D_{\parallel}-D_{\perp}}{2(3\mu-\nu)}[\mu(1+3M)+\nu(1-M)]\zeta\rho_{0}+\frac{(c_{1}+c_{2}\zeta\rho_{0})(1-\zeta\rho_{0})}{(3\mu-\nu)^{3}(3\kappa+\nu)}v^{2}\right]k_{x}^{2},

(147)

with
c_{1}=4\mu[-3\mu^{2}+\nu(2\mu+4\kappa+\nu)]
, and

c_{2}=\kappa\nu^{2}(1-M)+\mu[3\mu^{2}(7-3M)-2\kappa\nu(11+3M)-\nu^{2}(3+5M)+9\kappa\mu(1+3M)+2\mu\nu(-7+9M)].

Now we look at a perturbation in the
y
direction (
k_{x}=0
). At leading order in
k_{y}\ll 1
, the real part of the eigenvalues, calculated with Mathematica
2
, are:
\lambda_{\rm liq,y}^{1,2}=(3\kappa+\nu)/2
,
\lambda_{\rm liq,y}^{3}\simeq(3\mu+\nu)
, and

\displaystyle\lambda_{{\rm liq},y}^{4}\simeq\left[\frac{-(2D_{\parallel}+D_{\perp})\mu+D_{\perp}\nu}{3\mu-\nu}+\frac{D_{\parallel}-D_{\perp}}{2(3\mu-\nu)}[\mu(1+3M)+\nu(1-M)]\zeta\rho_{0}+\frac{(d_{1}+d_{2}\zeta\rho_{0})(1-\zeta\rho_{0})}{(3\mu-\nu)(3\kappa+\nu)}v^{2}\right]k_{y}^{2},

(148)

with
d_{1}=4\mu
, and
d_{2}=\mu(-7+3M)+\nu(1-M)
.

The ordered homogeneous solution is then stable if
3\kappa+\nu<0
and
3\mu-\nu<0
for the two different perturbations. This result was already observed for the unrestricted APM
4
, and allows the selection of the position magnetization solution:
M=M_{0}+M_{1}\delta
. However, the stability of the two different perturbations differs from
\lambda_{{\rm liq},x}^{4}
and
\lambda_{{\rm liq},y}^{4}
. The perturbation along
x
is stable only if

\displaystyle\lambda_{\parallel}=\frac{\lambda_{{\rm liq},x}^{4}}{k_{x}^{2}}=-D+\left[\frac{\mu+\nu}{3\mu-\nu}(1-\zeta\rho_{0})-M\zeta\rho_{0}\right]\frac{D\epsilon}{3}-\frac{(c_{1}+c_{2}\zeta\rho_{0})(1-\zeta\rho_{0})}{(3\mu-\nu)^{3}(3\kappa+\nu)}\left(\frac{4D\epsilon}{3}\right)^{2}

(149)

is negative and the perturbation along
y
is stable only if

\lambda_{\perp}=\frac{\lambda_{{\rm liq},y}^{4}}{k_{y}^{2}}=-D-\left[\frac{\mu+\nu}{3\mu-\nu}(1-\zeta\rho_{0})-M\zeta\rho_{0}\right]\frac{D\epsilon}{3}+\frac{(d_{1}+d_{2}\zeta\rho_{0})(1-\zeta\rho_{0})}{(3\mu-\nu)(3\kappa+\nu)}\left(\frac{4D\epsilon}{3}\right)^{2}

(150)

is negative. We may note that these eigenvalues are those obtained in Ref.
4
for
\zeta=0
. In Fig.
76
(b), we have represented the velocity-density stability diagram for
\beta=0.75
and
{\rm MPS}=2
according to the sign of these two eigenvalues. Here, we have not derived an analytical expression of
\epsilon_{*}
, for which the reorientation transition occurs, but we have computed a numerical estimation in Fig.
76
(c).
\epsilon_{*}
is an decreasing function of MPS, meaning that
\epsilon_{*}
increases with a strong repulsion.

4
Linear stability analysis for the soft-core rAPM

For soft-core rAPM, the hydrodynamic equation is

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{\sigma}=D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}\left[\exp(-s\rho)\left(\partial_{\parallel}\rho_{\sigma}+s\rho_{\sigma}\partial_{\parallel}\rho\right)\right]+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}\left[\exp(-s\rho)\left(\partial_{\perp}\rho_{\sigma}+s\rho_{\sigma}\partial_{\perp}\rho\right)\right]-v\partial_{\parallel}\left[\exp(-s\rho)\rho_{\sigma}\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\left[\frac{4\beta J}{\rho}(\rho_{\sigma}+\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1-\frac{r}{\rho}-\alpha\frac{(\rho_{\sigma}-\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right](\rho_{\sigma}-\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}),

(151)

with
\alpha=8(\beta J)^{2}(1-2\beta J/3)
and
s=2\beta U
. The homogeneous solutions are given by:

I_{\rm flip}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})=\left[\frac{4\beta J}{\rho}(\rho_{\sigma}+\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1-\frac{r}{\rho}-\alpha\frac{(\rho_{\sigma}-\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right](\rho_{\sigma}-\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})=0,

(152)

and are then those of the unrestricted rAPM
1
. The disordered homogeneous solution is
\rho_{\sigma}=\rho_{0}/4
, and the ordered homogeneous solution (supposed along state
\sigma=1
) is
\rho_{1}=\rho_{0}(1+3M)/4
and
\rho_{2,3,4}=\rho_{0}(1-M)/4
with the magnetization
M
following the equation:

2\beta J(1+M)-1-\frac{r}{\rho_{0}}-\alpha M^{2}=0,

(153)

or
M=M_{0}\pm M_{1}\delta
with
M_{0}=\beta J/\alpha
,
M_{1}=\sqrt{r/\alpha\rho_{*}}
and
\delta=\sqrt{(\rho_{0}-\rho_{*})/\rho_{0}}
, where
\rho_{*}
defined by

\rho_{*}=\frac{8(1-2\beta J/3)r}{1+8(2\beta J-1)(1-2\beta J/3)},

(154)

is the critical density below which the ordered homogeneous solution does not exist, for a temperature below
T_{c}=(1-\sqrt{22}/8)^{-1}\simeq 2.417
.

Linear stability analysis for the disordered homogeneous solution.
We take
\rho_{\sigma}=\rho_{0}/4+\delta\rho_{\sigma}
and
\rho=\rho_{0}+\delta\rho
, with
\delta\rho=\sum_{\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma}
. The hopping term writes

\displaystyle I_{\rm hop}\simeq\exp(-s\rho_{0})\left[\left(1+\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}\right)(D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2})-v\left(1-\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}\right)\partial_{\parallel}\right]\delta\rho_{\sigma}

\displaystyle+\exp(-s\rho_{0})\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}\left[D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2}+v\partial_{\parallel}\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},

(155)

and the flipping term writes
I_{\rm flip}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\simeq\mu_{0}(\delta\rho_{\sigma}-\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})
, with
\mu_{0}=2\beta J-1-r/\rho_{0}
. Then, in the Fourier space, the hydrodynamic equation becomes

\partial_{t}\delta\rho_{\sigma}=\left[A(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})+3\mu_{0}\right]\delta\rho_{\sigma}+\left[B(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})-\mu_{0}\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},

(156)

with

\displaystyle A(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\exp(-s\rho_{0})\left[\left(1+\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}\right)(-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2})+\imath k_{\parallel}v\left(1-\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}\right)\right]

(157)

\displaystyle B(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\exp(-s\rho_{0})\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}\left[-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}-\imath k_{\parallel}v\right]

(158)

The stability of the homogeneous disordered solution is then given by the eigenvalues of the matrix

M_{\rm gas}=\begin{pmatrix}A(k_{x},k_{y})+3\mu_{0}&B(k_{x},k_{y})-\mu_{0}&B(k_{x},k_{y})-\mu_{0}&B(k_{x},k_{y})-\mu_{0}\\
B(k_{y},-k_{x})-\mu_{0}&A(k_{y},-k_{x})+3\mu_{0}&B(k_{y},-k_{x})-\mu_{0}&B(k_{y},-k_{x})-\mu_{0}\\
B(-k_{x},-k_{y})-\mu_{0}&B(-k_{x},-k_{y})-\mu_{0}&A(-k_{x},-k_{y})+3\mu_{0}&B(-k_{x},-k_{y})-\mu_{0}\\
B(-k_{y},k_{x})-\mu_{0}&B(-k_{y},k_{x})-\mu_{0}&B(-k_{y},k_{x})-\mu_{0}&A(-k_{y},k_{x})+3\mu_{0}\end{pmatrix}.

(159)

Supposing
k_{x}=k
and
k_{y}=0
(since no preferred direction), at leading order in
k\ll 1
, the eigenvalues, calculated with Mathematica
2
, are:
\lambda_{\rm gas}^{1,2,3}\simeq 4\mu_{0}
and

\displaystyle\lambda_{\rm gas}^{4}\simeq\exp(-s\rho_{0})\left[-(1+s\rho_{0})\frac{D_{\parallel}+D_{\perp}}{2}+(1-s\rho_{0})\exp(-s\rho_{0})\frac{v^{2}}{8\mu_{0}}\right]k^{2}.

(160)

The disordered homogeneous solution is then stable if
\mu_{0}<0
and

\lambda_{\rm gas}=-(1+s\rho_{0})D+(1-s\rho_{0})\exp(-s\rho_{0})\frac{v^{2}}{8\mu_{0}}<0.

(161)

In Fig.
77
(a), we have represented the velocity-density stability diagram for
\beta=0.75
and
U=0.5
according to the sign of this eigenvalue. Note that the last inequality is always fulfilled when
U=0
, meaning that
\mu_{0}<0
only impacts the spinodals:
\varphi_{\rm gas}(U=0)=r/(2\beta J-1)
, independent of
\epsilon
. However, we cannot extract an analytical expression for the spinodal
\varphi_{\rm gas}
for all
U
values.

Figure 77:
(Color online)
(a)
and
(b)
Velocity-density stability diagram of the rAPM with soft-core repulsion for the disordered and ordered homogeneous solution for
\beta=0.75
and
U=0.5
.
(a)
The stability region of the disordered solution is plotted in blue, according to the eigenvalue
\lambda_{\rm gas}
given by Eq. (
161
).
(b)
The stability region of the ordered solution is plotted in blue, according to the eigenvalues
\lambda_{\parallel}
and
\lambda_{\perp}
given by Eqs. (
176
) and (
4
), respectively. The ordered solution is only unstable under longitudinal perturbations (
\lambda_{\parallel}>0
) in the light blue region, and under transverse perturbations (
\lambda_{\perp}>0
) in the light red region.
(c)

\epsilon_{*}
value for which the reorientation transition occurs, as a function of
U
for several temperatures
T=\beta^{-1}
.

Linear stability analysis for the ordered homogeneous solution.
We consider the ordered solution along the right state, and we take
\rho_{1}=\rho_{0}(1+3M)/4+\delta\rho_{1}
,
\rho_{2,3,4}=\rho_{0}(1+3M)/4+\delta\rho_{2,3,4}
and
\rho=\rho_{0}+\delta\rho
, with
\delta\rho=\sum_{\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma}
. The hopping term of the right state
\sigma=1
writes

\displaystyle I_{\rm hop}^{(1)}\simeq\exp(-s\rho_{0})\left\{\left[1+\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1+3M)\right](D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2})-v\left[1-\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1+3M)\right]\partial_{\parallel}\right\}\delta\rho_{\sigma}

\displaystyle+\exp(-s\rho_{0})\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1+3M)\left[D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2}+v\partial_{\parallel}\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},

(162)

and the hopping term of the other states
\sigma\neq 1
writes

\displaystyle I_{\rm hop}^{(2)}\simeq\exp(-s\rho_{0})\left\{\left[1+\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1-M)\right](D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2})-v\left[1-\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1-M)\right]\partial_{\parallel}\right\}\delta\rho_{\sigma}

\displaystyle+\exp(-s\rho_{0})\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1-M)\left[D_{\parallel}\partial_{\parallel}^{2}+D_{\perp}\partial_{\perp}^{2}+v\partial_{\parallel}\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}.

(163)

We may note for
M=0
, we recover the expression of
I_{\rm hop}
, given by Eq. (
155
), calculated for the disordered solution.

The flipping terms implying the right state write

I_{\rm flip}(1,\sigma^{\prime})\simeq M\left\{(4\beta J-2\alpha M)\delta\rho_{1}+(4\beta J+2\alpha M)\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}-\left[2\beta J(1+M)-\frac{r}{\rho_{0}}-2\alpha M^{2}\right]\delta\rho\right\}.

(164)

Using Eq. (
153
), we get

\displaystyle I_{\rm flip}(1,\sigma^{\prime})\simeq M\left[(4\beta J-2\alpha M)\delta\rho_{1}+(4\beta J+2\alpha M)\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}+(\alpha M^{2}-1)\delta\rho\right]

\displaystyle\equiv\gamma_{1}\delta\rho_{1}+\gamma_{2}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}+\gamma_{3}\delta\rho.

(165)

The flipping term which does not imply the right state write

I_{\rm flip}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\simeq M(\alpha M-4\beta J)(\delta\rho_{\sigma}-\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}})\equiv\gamma_{4}(\delta\rho_{\sigma}-\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}).

(166)

Then we have the terms:

I_{\sigma}=\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}I_{\rm flip}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\simeq\begin{cases}3(\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{3})\delta\rho_{\sigma}+(\gamma_{2}+3\gamma_{3})\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},\hskip 18.49988pt&{\rm if}\penalty\ \sigma=1,\\
-(\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{3})\delta\rho_{1}+(-\gamma_{2}-\gamma_{3}+2\gamma_{4})\delta\rho_{\sigma}-(\gamma_{3}+\gamma_{4})\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\{1,\sigma\}}\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},\hskip 18.49988pt&{\rm if}\penalty\ \sigma\neq 1.\end{cases}

(167)

Then, in the Fourier space, the hydrodynamic equation becomes

\partial_{t}\delta\rho_{\sigma}=\begin{cases}\left[A_{1}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})+3\mu\right]\delta\rho_{\sigma}+\left[B_{1}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})+\nu\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},\hskip 18.49988pt&{\rm if}\penalty\ \sigma=1,\\
\left[A_{2}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})+\kappa\right]\delta\rho_{\sigma}+\left[B_{2}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})-\mu\right]\delta\rho_{1}+\left[B_{2}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})-\frac{\kappa+\nu}{2}\right]\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\{1,\sigma\}}\delta\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}},\hskip 18.49988pt&{\rm if}\penalty\ \sigma\neq 1,\end{cases}

(168)

with
\mu=\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{3}=M(4\beta J-2\alpha M+\alpha M^{2}-1)
,
\nu=\gamma_{2}+3\gamma_{3}=M(4\beta J+2\alpha M+3\alpha M^{2}-3)
,
\kappa=-\gamma_{2}-\gamma_{3}+2\gamma_{4}=M(-12\beta J-\alpha M^{2}+1)
, and

\displaystyle A_{1}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\exp(-s\rho_{0})\left\{\left[1+\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1+3M)\right](-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2})+\imath k_{\parallel}v\left[1-\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1+3M)\right]\right\},

(169)

\displaystyle B_{1}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\exp(-s\rho_{0})\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1+3M)\left[-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}-\imath k_{\parallel}v\right],

(170)

\displaystyle A_{2}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\exp(-s\rho_{0})\left\{\left[1+\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1-M)\right](-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2})+\imath k_{\parallel}v\left[1-\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1-M)\right]\right\},

(171)

\displaystyle B_{2}(k_{\parallel},k_{\perp})=\exp(-s\rho_{0})\frac{s\rho_{0}}{4}(1-M)\left[-D_{\parallel}k_{\parallel}^{2}-D_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}-\imath k_{\parallel}v\right].

(172)

The stability of the homogeneous ordered solution is then given by the eigenvalues of the matrix

M_{\rm liq}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{1}(k_{x},k_{y})+3\mu&B_{1}(k_{x},k_{y})+\nu&B_{1}(k_{x},k_{y})+\nu&B_{1}(k_{x},k_{y})+\nu\\
B_{2}(k_{y},-k_{x})-\mu&A_{2}(k_{y},-k_{x})+\kappa&B_{2}(k_{y},-k_{x})-(\kappa+\nu)/2&B_{2}(k_{y},-k_{x})-(\kappa+\nu)/2\\
B_{2}(-k_{x},-k_{y})-\mu&B_{2}(-k_{x},-k_{y})-(\kappa+\nu)/2&A_{2}(-k_{x},-k_{y})+\kappa&B_{2}(-k_{x},-k_{y})-(\kappa+\nu)/2\\
B_{2}(-k_{y},k_{x})-\mu&B_{2}(-k_{y},k_{x})-(\kappa+\nu)/2&B_{2}(-k_{y},k_{x})-(\kappa+\nu)/2&A_{2}(-k_{y},k_{x})+\kappa\end{pmatrix}.

(173)

In the following, we will denote
\overline{D_{\parallel}}=D_{\parallel}\exp(-s\rho_{0})
,
\overline{D_{\perp}}=D_{\perp}\exp(-s\rho_{0})
and
\overline{v}=v\exp(-s\rho_{0})
. First, we consider a perturbation in the
x
direction (
k_{y}=0
). At leading order in
k_{x}\ll 1
, the real part of the eigenvalues, calculated with Mathematica
2
, are:
\lambda_{\rm liq,x}^{1,2}\simeq(3\kappa+\nu)/2
,
\lambda_{\rm liq,x}^{3}\simeq(3\mu+\nu)
, and

\displaystyle\lambda_{{\rm liq},x}^{4}\simeq-\left[\frac{(\overline{D_{\parallel}}+2\overline{D_{\perp}})\mu-\overline{D_{\parallel}}\nu}{3\mu-\nu}+\frac{\overline{D_{\parallel}}(1+M)+\overline{D_{\perp}}(1-M)}{2}s\rho_{0}+\frac{c_{1}+c_{2}s\rho_{0}}{(3\mu-\nu)^{3}(3\kappa+\nu)}\overline{v}^{2}\right]k_{x}^{2},

(174)

with
c_{1}=4\mu[-3\mu^{2}+\nu(2\mu+4\kappa+\nu)]
, and
c_{2}=(3\mu-\nu)\{3\mu[\kappa(1+3M)+\mu(1-M)]-\nu[\kappa(1-M)+\mu(1-5M)]\}
.

Now we look at a perturbation in the
y
direction (
k_{x}=0
). At leading order in
k_{y}\ll 1
, the real part of the eigenvalues, calculated with Mathematica
2
, are:
\lambda_{\rm liq,y}^{1,2}\simeq(3\kappa+\nu)/2
,
\lambda_{\rm liq,y}^{3}\simeq(3\mu+\nu)
, and

\lambda_{{\rm liq},y}^{4}\simeq\left[\frac{-(2\overline{D_{\parallel}}+\overline{D_{\perp}})\mu+\overline{D_{\perp}}\nu}{3\mu-\nu}-\frac{\overline{D_{\parallel}}(1-M)+\overline{D_{\perp}}(1+M)}{2}s\rho_{0}+\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}s\rho_{0}}{(3\mu-\nu)(3\kappa+\nu)}\overline{v}^{2}\right]k_{y}^{2},

(175)

with
d_{1}=4\mu
, and
d_{2}=-(1-M)(3\mu-\nu)
.

The ordered homogeneous solution is then stable if
3\kappa+\nu<0
and
3\mu-\nu<0
for the two different perturbations. This result was already observed for the unrestricted APM
4
, and allows the selection of the position magnetization solution:
M=M_{0}+M_{1}\delta
. However, the stability of the two different perturbations differs from
\lambda_{{\rm liq},x}^{4}
and
\lambda_{{\rm liq},y}^{4}
. The perturbation along
x
is stable only if

\displaystyle\lambda_{\parallel}=\frac{\lambda_{{\rm liq},x}^{4}}{k_{x}^{2}\exp(-s\rho_{0})}=-D(1+s\rho_{0})+\left[\frac{\mu+\nu}{3\mu-\nu}-Ms\rho_{0}\right]\frac{D\epsilon}{3}

\displaystyle-\frac{(c_{1}+c_{2}s\rho_{0})\exp(-s\rho_{0})}{(3\mu-\nu)^{3}(3\kappa+\nu)}\left(\frac{4D\epsilon}{3}\right)^{2}

(176)

is negative and the perturbation along
y
is stable only if

\displaystyle\lambda_{\perp}=\frac{\lambda_{{\rm liq},y}^{4}}{k_{y}^{2}\exp(-s\rho_{0})}=-D(1+s\rho_{0})-\left[\frac{\mu+\nu}{3\mu-\nu}-Ms\rho_{0}\right]\frac{D\epsilon}{3}

\displaystyle+\frac{(d_{1}+d_{2}s\rho_{0})\exp(-s\rho_{0})}{(3\mu-\nu)(3\kappa+\nu)}\left(\frac{4D\epsilon}{3}\right)^{2}

(177)

is negative. We may note that these eigenvalues are those obtained in Ref.
4
for
s=0
. In Fig.
77
(b), we have represented the velocity-density stability diagram for
\beta=0.75
and
U=0.5
according to the sign of these two eigenvalues. Here, we have not derived an analytical expression of
\epsilon_{*}
, for which the reorientation transition occurs, but we have computed a numerical estimation in Fig.
77
(c).
\epsilon_{*}(U)
increases for small
U
and decreases to zero at large
U
.
